What is at stakenothing externalmarriage, child, work

Delivery and cost do not settle it — they reverse with the weighting. Three things do, because none depends on a scoring choice: the target moved, the payout channel inverted, and career conversion is higher ahead on both weightings.

The past produced a person. Aimed at the family, paid out through the graha of release, with nothing external to lose and almost no conversion into the world. It burned things away and left someone who works alone under load. That is formation.

The future produces a life. Aimed at the self and the work, three Saturn mechanisms instead of one, a marriage and a child and a career attached to the outcome, and the highest career conversion of anything before 2040. That is conversion.

And the deepest difference is the one with no arithmetic in it:

What the past took, Surya gave back as authority and healing. What the future takes, Ketu does not give back — it removes the wanting.

So the honest answer is not that one is harder. §19 established they are comparable in weight. It is that the past cost him things he had not chosen and returned a self. The future costs him things he will have chosen and returns a role.

One built the instrument. The other plays it, and charges admission.

The decisive asymmetry — maturity

GrahaMaturesYearIn the past window?In the window ahead?
Guru162018yes—
Surya222024——
Chandra242026——
Shukra252027—yes
Mangal282030—yes
Budha322034——
Shani362038——

The Mangal mahadasha ran before Mangal itself matured.

Mangal matures at 28, in 2030. Its mahadasha ended in December 2022 — more than seven years before the graha came into its own strength. The 8th lord governed seven years of his life while it was still, in the classical sense, not yet itself.

And Mangal's maturation falls inside the coming window — 2030, the exact peak year the forward scoring identified independently.

One graha did mature during the past window: Guru, in 2018 — the one benefic in a kendra, the Amala giver. Worth noting on its own. The only maturation available to him during his hardest early stretch was the graha of protection and reputation. Two mature inside or beside the coming one: Shukra in 2027 and Mangal in 2030 — the Atmakaraka and the 8th lord itself.

The other asymmetry, stated as what it is

Jyotisha describes structure, not agency, so this is an observation about the person rather than a computation.

Past window, ages 13.7 to 20.7: no position, no resources, no independent household, no professional standing. The 8th arrived and there was nothing to do but undergo it.

Window ahead, ages 25.1 to 31.0: by the chart's own timeline — married by early 2028, the career-foundation antardasha opening the same week, a child around 2029, Shukra matured in 2027.

The same house, at an age with something to lose and something to steer.

What this changes

1. The coming window is not his first, and this document should stop implying it is. That language has been corrected wherever it appeared.

2. It is not clearly the harder of the two. Which is heavier depends on a scoring choice that can go either way. What is certain: the coming window carries three markers the past one did not — the Saturn return, Sade Sati and the Bhrigu Bindu crossing — while the past one carried two the coming one does not: the 8th lord's own mahadasha, and the Rahu return.

3. A seventh blind spot, and it is new. §55 lists six. This is the seventh, and it is the only one that exists because of something he has lived:

He has survived an 8th-house passage already, and the natural inference — "I have been through this, I know what it feels like" — is wrong here, because the instruments differ.

The past window was the 8th lord acting from inside, at maximum delivery capacity, with little at stake externally. The coming one is transit pressure on a matured apparatus with a marriage, a child and a career attached. Pattern-matching the second to the first will understate it.

4. It makes one piece of counsel actionable for the first time. "Change position, don't just push harder" was written for a man who, at fourteen, had no position to change. At twenty-eight he will have one. The instruction becomes usable exactly when the chart most needs it to be.

5. And one reassurance that is earned rather than offered. He came through seven years governed by the 8th lord at the highest delivery capacity in the chart, with only Guru matured and no resources of his own. D27's zero dusthana occupancy said the transformations were survivable.

The past window is the first evidence that they actually were — and it is the only evidence anywhere in this document that comes from his life rather than from his chart.

20 The same thing, in plain words

The last two sections are dense and technical. This one says the same thing in ordinary words. Nothing new is claimed here — every statement below is a plain-language version of something computed in §18 or §19, and the numbers are there if you want them.

One thing to hold on to before starting: the chart describes a shape, not a script. It says what kind of pressure arrives and roughly when. It does not say what actually happened to a real person, and where this section sounds like it is describing events, read it as "this is the sort of thing that period is built to produce."

What already happened — roughly ages 14 to 21

The part of his chart that governs upheaval was in charge of those seven years. Not visiting. In charge. From late 2015 to late 2022.

It was pointed at the family, not at him. That is unusual and it is specific: the same influence, in a different chart, would have gone after health or career. Here it went after home, the father, and the conditions of his schooling.

The heaviest part was the middle, not the beginning. Roughly ages 15 to 18, there was a slow grinding pressure — three straight years of it. Not a single dramatic event. Something more like weight that does not lift.

At 17 or 18 he was asked the question everyone gets asked at that age — who are you, and what are you going to do — and the part of his chart that answers that question is the weakest thing he owns. So the question got forced early and the answer was not available. That is not a character flaw. It is a structural mismatch of timing.

He had nothing external to lose. No job, no household, no standing, no reputation. Whatever that period took, it could not take those, because he did not have them yet.

And what it took, it gave back as self-reliance. The technical reason is in §19; the plain version is that this particular kind of pressure, in this particular chart, converts into capability rather than just damage. He came out of it able to work alone, under load, without anyone holding the structure up for him. That is the single most useful thing he owns, and it was installed in those years.

It ended better than it ran. The last year and a half of that period — roughly 18½ to 20 — was the good part. The turn came at the end.

What is coming — roughly ages 25 to 31

Same area of life gets worked on. Completely different tool.

Last time the pressure came from inside — the ruling period of those years was itself the upheaval. This time it comes from outside: slow planetary transits pressing on it while the ruling period is something else entirely. Before, the house was acting. Now, the house is being acted upon.

It is pointed at him and his work, not at his family. Identity, career, health, children. The family recedes; the self and the job come forward.

There are three sources of pressure instead of one. The earlier window had one. This one has three overlapping — and they start earlier than this document originally said: June 2027, not late 2027.

Things will be attached to the outcome this time. By the chart's own timeline: a marriage around early 2028, a career foundation beginning the same week, possibly a child around 2029. He will have things that can be lost. Last time he did not.

The same question gets asked again — and by the same weak instrument. Around December 2030 to mid-2033, the identical part of the chart that handled the who-are-you question at seventeen handles it again. Except now there is a marriage, a child and a career attached to the answer.

But this time it converts. This is the one measurement that holds no matter how you calculate it: the coming window turns pressure into position and standing in a way the earlier one structurally could not. The earlier one produced a person. This one produces a life.

And it also ends better than it runs. From about 2033 to 2037 is the material peak of his thirties.

The difference in what comes out

Before, 14–21Ahead, 25–31
Where the pressure landshome, father, schoolinghimself, his work, his marriage
Where it comes frominside — the period itselfoutside — slow transits
How many sourcesonethree
What he had to losenothing externala marriage, a child, a career
What it buildsa persona position
What it hands backself-reliance and authorityit takes the wanting away instead

That last row is the deepest difference and it is worth sitting with.

The earlier period was governed by an influence that pays out through the part of the chart associated with healing and authority. So what it burned away, it returned in another form. He got something back.

The coming period's central stretch pays out through the part of the chart associated with dissolution — and that part does not hand things back. It removes the wanting instead. You do not end up compensated. You end up no longer needing the thing.

Whether that reads as bleak or as a relief depends entirely on where you are standing when it happens.

So, in one line each:

The past cost him things he had not chosen, and gave him back a self.

The future will cost him things he will have chosen, and give him back a role.

How to handle it differently

This is the part that matters, and there are seven things.

1. Do not assume it will feel like last time. He has survived one of these. The natural instinct will be "I know what this is." He does not — the mechanism is different, the target is different, and he has things at stake now that did not exist before. This is listed in §55 as his seventh blind spot, and it is the only one on that list that exists because of something he has actually lived.

2. Last time there was nothing to protect. This time there is. The clear window closes 3 June 2027 — about nine months from now. Whatever is worth securing, secure it before then: the relationship, the health routine, the working relationships that will matter. Not because disaster is coming, but because the last time this happened he had nothing to arrange and this time he does.

3. Last time he endured. This time he can steer. At fourteen he had no choices. At twenty-eight he will have them, and the chart's standing advice — change where you stand rather than just working harder — becomes executable for the first time. He is built to work harder. That will be the wrong instrument again, and it will feel like the right one.

4. Expect the middle to be the worst, not the start. Both windows put the hardest stretch in the middle. Around 2030 to 2031 is the equivalent of the earlier 15-to-18 stretch. Knowing that in advance is worth something: it means the difficult part arriving is a schedule, not a verdict.

5. The turn comes at the end, both times. Last time the good part was the final eighteen months. This time it is 2033 to 2037. He should not read the middle of a period as its conclusion — he did that at seventeen and had no way of knowing better.

6. Do not measure it by how it feels. His chart carries a built-in sense of incompleteness — it attaches to whatever he obtains, regardless of whether the thing is good. The feeling is not the evidence. This applies to the marriage most of all, and §44 says so at length: his dissatisfaction, where it appears, is not evidence about her.

7. He will not be doing it alone this time, and that is the actual difference. Last time he was alone because there was no one else in the structure. The chart's reading of the marriage is that it is durable and undemonstrative — it holds without being effusive. That is exactly the kind of thing that is worth almost nothing at twenty-two and worth a great deal at thirty.

And the honest limits, plainly

  • None of this is a prediction of events. It describes the kind of pressure a period is built to produce, and the timing of it.
  • The only part of this document that has been tested against a real life is the past window — and even that is offered for him to check, not asserted.
  • The dates are exact; the descriptions are not. Sade Sati begins 3 June 2027 and the Saturn return is 2 June 2031. What those mean in a life is interpretation.
  • This is Jyotisha on its own terms. It is a framework with internal consistency. Nothing here demonstrates that the framework is true, and this document has never claimed otherwise.
  • And the reading has been wrong before. It has corrected itself eight or nine times in this document, including twice about this very period. Treat it as a careful argument, not as an oracle.

21 The role, specifically — and why it is a different kind

§19 closed on a sentence: the past cost him things he had not chosen and returned a self; the future costs him things he will have chosen and returns a role. That leaves the obvious question unanswered. Which role?

The word was doing real work in that sentence, so verify_role.py makes it earn it. Three things have to hold: that the past did not give him roles (or the distinction collapses), that the chart names particular roles for the coming window, and that those roles carry a specific kind of authority. Nothing here is a new prediction — every date below is already in this document. This names what occupies them.

First: did the past give him roles too?

The roles available between ages 13.7 and 20.7 are son, student and brother. The chart's own numbers for the houses that carry them:

HouseSignSAVBhava rankOccupants
9father / sonVrishabha227Chandra, Mangal, Shani, Rahu
3siblings, effortVrischika288Ketu — severest gandanta pada
4mother, home, schoolingDhanu292empty
5education, mindMakara296empty

A correction to what this section was first drafted as saying. I had written that the Mangal years put all four of those under load. They do not. Only two carry direct weight — the 9th, at the chart's second-lowest SAV with four grahas including Rahu in Marana Karaka Sthana, and the 3rd, holding gandanta Ketu. The 4th is rank 2 of 12 and empty; the 5th is rank 6 and empty. Both are well built and receive nothing worse than Mangal's aspect.

That is a more precise account of those years than the reading had: the pressure fell on the father, and on siblings-and-effort — not on home and schooling as such, which is also why the domestic base came through it intact.

But the finding does not depend on all four being weak. It depends on all four being assigned. Son, student, brother — every one of them is a position you are put in. None is taken.

The past gave him no role. It gave him positions he was born into, and put the two that carried his father and his effort under load. What he came out with was a capability — the ability to work alone under pressure. That is a property of a person, not a position.

What the coming window names

RoleWhenHouse
HUSBANDRahu–Guru, to 31 January 20287th — MeenaSAV 33rank 4
THE ONE RESPONSIBLERahu–Shani, 31 Jan 2028 – 7 Dec 203010th — MithunaSAV 29rank 9
FATHERinside Shani–Shukra, Feb–Jul 20295th — MakaraSAV 29rank 6

Husband. Guru rules both the 7th and the 4th and runs the antardasha; transit Shani sits in the natal 7th until 3 June 2027; transit Guru aspects it. Three independent activators of the 7th at once — the clearest such window in the visible timeline.

The one responsible. Shani carries four career credentials simultaneously — D10 lagna lord, Amatyakaraka, lord of the 41-bindu 6th, and occupant of D9's 10th. Nothing else in the chart carries more than two. This is the sub-period the reading has called the foundation from the beginning.

Father. Shani rules the 5th and runs the antardasha, Shukra is the Atmakaraka, and transit Guru crosses the natal lagna across 2028. The 5th is loaded far more heavily here than in the past window.

All three are roles he enters rather than inherits. That is what the word in §19 was carrying, and it holds: son, student and brother were assigned; husband, office-holder and father are taken on.

What kind of authority — and it is not the obvious kind

Here the chart is unusually specific, and it says something most readings would soften. Four measurements, all pointing the same way.

1. The 10th lord ranks fourth as a career agent in his own chart. Budha rules the 10th and scores 3.77. Shani, which rules nothing in the career houses, scores 7.96. The graha with the title does not have the power.

2. No Panchamahapurusha yoga. With one kendra occupied, and that by a graha in an enemy sign, none of the five great-person yogas can form. Nothing in this chart confers stature automatically.

3. Both Jaimini authority indicators land on the empty 12th — and the 12th is the strongest bhava in the chart. A second correction to the draft: I read rank 1 as weakness. It is the opposite. 12.59 rupas, rank 1 of 12, the best-built house he owns, and empty — while the 10th itself ranks 9 of 12. So his seat of public authority is real and it is located out of sight: foreign, secluded, behind the scenes. Not absent. Unwitnessed.

4. The 6th carries 41 bindus, the highest of any sign, and the D10 ascendant is that same sign. The house of service, competition and applied problem-solving is the strongest thing he owns by that measure.

The role is responsibility without title.

He becomes the person things are handed to — not the person whose name is on the door. The chart is emphatic that the second is not available and does not become available on its own. The one who is called when it is broken, contested or opaque, and who is trusted to resolve it.

That is the authority of the expert and the trusted hand, and it is exactly what a Kumbha D10 ascendant under Shani, a 41-bindu 6th, and Amala Yoga describe between them.

All three roles are the same role

This is the finding, and it took naming all three to see it.

Husbandsomeone depends on him and he is not free to leave
The one responsiblesomething depends on him and he is not free to hand it back
Fathersomeone depends on him absolutely, and there is no version where he is not responsible

All three are the same structure: being the one who does not get to put it down. And the chart arranges them in ascending order of irrevocability, across thirty months.

Set that against what §48 established — that this is a chart whose central mechanism is setting things down, and whose only occupant of the three houses of desire is the graha that removes attachment.

That is the real difficulty of the coming window, and it is not the hardship. It is that a man built to release is being handed, in the space of thirty months, three things he structurally cannot release.

And what makes it survivable

Three answers, each found for other reasons and worth collecting here.

  1. The 4th is rank 2 and the Upapada sits in it. The marriage attaches to the second-strongest house in the chart. Whatever else is strained, the domestic base is well built — the same rank-2 4th that came through the Mangal years intact.
  2. The marriage is durable precisely because its dissolution-house is weak. §54 found the 8th from the Upapada is his 11th — rank 11 of 12, carrying both Gulika and Mandi. The house that would end the marriage is too feeble to act. The same configuration that thins his friendships is what makes the marriage hard to break.
  3. Shani and Guru are the only grahas in adult avastha, and they govern everything from 38.7 onward. The parts of him equipped for sustained obligation are precisely the parts that have already grown up.

Qualified by §32. That reads the Baladi scheme, which reports both simply as adult. A second avastha scheme — Lajjitadi, computed later — separates them sharply: Shani comes out Mudita, delighted; Guru comes out Kshudita, starved, and is the only graha in the chart that is neither proud nor delighted. The point stands, but Shani carries it and Guru does not — and the scheme this section originally used could not see the difference.

So the chart hands a releaser three unreleasable things, and then supplies the two grahas of endurance to carry them. That is the arrangement. Not comfortable, and not incoherent.

One honest qualification. None of this predicts that a marriage or a child occurs. It says the chart activates those houses on those dates, and that the roles it names are of a particular kind. Whether a life fills them is not something Jyotisha can settle.

22 Career and earning through a job — how it grows

§54 already settled the mode: this chart is built for employment inside an institution, not for proprietorship. That leaves the question actually asked, which is different and had never been computed — if he is in a job now, how does it grow, and what does the chart say about the money?

verify_earning.py separates four things the reading had been lumping together, because this chart treats them very differently:

ChannelHouse
STANDINGposition, title, what the work is10th
SERVICEthe employment relation itself, competition6th
INCOMEgains arriving — the flow11th
RETENTIONwhat stays2nd

Nothing here is a new dasha. Every date already exists in this document. What is new is the separation of the channels, and the growth curve across them.

Where he stands right now

Today is 19 August 2026, age 24.3 — in Rahu–Guru, which runs Sep 2025 – Jan 2028, inside the Rahu mahadasha (Dec 2022 – Dec 2040).

A job taken now attaches to Rahu–Guru, and Guru is not a neutral marker here. It occupies the 10th and forms Amala Yoga there, rules the 7th and the 4th, scores 5.89 as a career agent — rank 2 of 9, carries a net balance of +22.20, and matured at 16, so it runs at full strength. Transit Guru is crossing the 11th, the income house, from August 2026 to June 2027.

The entry point is genuinely good — and it is the last antardasha of its kind before the structural window opens. This is the on-ramp, not the destination.

The four channels are not the same strength

ChannelSignSAVBhava rankLordSPNet
10STANDINGMithuna299Budha152−11.41
6SERVICEKumbha4110Shani184−34.35
11INCOMEKarka2811Chandra33+20.05
2RETENTIONTula243Shukra95+35.62

SERVICE is the strongest thing he owns. SAV 41 is the highest of any sign in the chart, and the D10 ascendant is that same sign. The employment relation itself is the strong part.

RETENTION is well served by one measure and not the other, and that has to be said rather than smoothed. The 2nd ranks 3 of 12 by Bhava Bala and its lord Shukra carries the best net balance in the chart and is the Atmakaraka — but its SAV is only 24, joint third-lowest of the twelve. Structurally sound, thinly supplied. The vessel is well made; the bindus say it does not fill quickly.

INCOME is the weak link, and it is weak twice over. The 11th ranks 11 of 12 and carries both Gulika and Mandi; its lord Chandra has Shodhya Pinda 33 — the lowest in the chart — and a career score of 0.14, also the lowest.

STANDING is middling. The 10th ranks 9, and its lord Budha is combust in the 8th — the only graha failing its Shadbala minimum.

He is strong at doing the job, strong at keeping what arrives, weak at the flow arriving in the first place, and the title is the least supported of the four.

That is not a poor chart for earning. It is a specific one: income grows slowly and is retained well, rather than arriving fast and dispersing.

So how does the money actually arrive?

If the flow-house is the weak one, the money comes from somewhere else — and the chart is specific. Shukra, the 2nd lord, sits in the 8th house, with the highest Ishta Phala and the best net balance in the chart.

The 8th is other people's resources, and things that arrive as events rather than as accrual: bonuses, equity that vests, settlements, gratuity, an inheritance, a payout attached to an occasion. Set that beside an 11th at rank 11 with both shadow points and the prediction is not ambiguous:

Salary accretion is the weak channel. Lump arrivals attached to events are the strong one.

The base rises slowly and unspectacularly; the real jumps come from things that are not the annual increment. His increment is not where his money is.

And the 6th — the competition house — is the strongest one. A 6th at 41 bindus against a 10th that is merely ordinary says growth comes from contests won, not from position held: applying for the thing, being measured against others and coming out ahead, changing employer. The weak 11th says the same thing from the other side — gains do not flow to him because he is there.

His raises come from moving, not from waiting.

Career score is the document's existing figure for the antardasha lord. Income is built in the script and shown transparently: ruling the 2nd or 11th +3 each, occupying them +2 each, Shodhya Pinda 0–3, net ±2 scaled, dhana karaka +1.

Note the row that matters most. Rahu–Shani carries the highest career score in the visible timeline and the lowest income score — 7.96 against 0.84. The period that builds the career pays the least while it is running.

(One caution on Rahu–Chandra: its income score of 4.49 comes almost entirely from Chandra ruling the 11th. The same Chandra has the lowest delivery in the chart. Rulership without delivery — read it as a favourable channel that supplies thinly.)

The two maturities that set the timetable

This needed no new computation — only putting the maturity table beside the career table.

Neither is mature yet. He is 24.

  • The career-defining antardasha (Rahu–Shani, Jan 2028 – Dec 2030) runs on a graha that will not be mature for another eight years.
  • The career hinge (Rahu–Budha, Dec 2030 – Jun 2033) runs on a graha that does not mature until Apr 2034 — about ten months after that period closes.

A correction to the draft. I had written that Budha matures inside its own antardasha. It does not, and the dates say so. The true version is worse for that period and better for what follows: the career hinge is run by Budha and finishes before Budha is ready.

That is the shape of the growth curve. Work laid down before either instrument is ready, paying out after they are. The chart is not describing a career that compounds smoothly from a job taken now. It describes foundation first, return later — with the crossover at April 2034.

The convergence is Rahu–Shukra, Jul 2034 – Jul 2037. Four independent things land on that window, and none was chosen to make them agree:

  1. the highest income score of any antardasha in the visible timeline
  2. transit Shani crossing the 11th, the income house, Jul 2034 – Aug 2036
  3. Budha — the 10th and lagna lord — maturing April 2034
  4. the document's existing finding, reached by entirely different means, that 2033–2037 is the material peak of his thirties

That is the answer to "how will it grow", and it is not the comfortable one: the career-defining work happens 2028–2030, and the money shows up about four years after it.

The second-ranked window is the one he is in. Rahu–Guru scores 9.17 with transit Guru on the income house to June 2027 — the near term is well-supported. It is the stretch after it, 2028–2033, that is heavy on construction and light on return.

Against what §45 already found

§45 answered "career growth" from the chart alone, with no premise about a job. It is worth checking whether this section, built on a different premise and a different apparatus, agrees with it. It does, on three points reached independently:

  • §45: "Rahu–Shukra and Rahu–Surya are a money and recognition window, not a position window." §22 finds those two antardashas hold the top two income scores and that Rahu–Shukra is where all four convergences land.
  • §45: "advancement through demonstrated competence, not through position." §22 finds the 6th outranks the 10th and the 10th lord ranks fourth.
  • §45: "growth is real, late, and stepped." §22 finds both career grahas mature at 32 and 36, and puts the income crossover at April 2034.

And they disagree about nothing, but they measure different things. §45's big step is December 2040, when the Guru mahadasha opens — beyond this section's horizon. §22 is about the shape inside the Rahu mahadasha.

Put together: money arrives 2034–2037, position arrives from 2040. The job he holds now is the instrument for the first, not yet for the second.

What this does not say

  1. No amount. Jyotisha does not produce a salary figure, and this reading has refused numeric claims everywhere else.
  2. No guarantee of the job itself. The premise came from him. The chart is being asked what happens to it, not whether it exists.
  3. The weak 11th is a statement about flow, not about poverty. The 2nd ranks 3 and its lord has the best net balance in the chart. A slow-filling vessel that does not leak is not an empty one.
  4. And the reading's standing advice applies exactly here. The 6th outranks the 10th, so changing where he stands beats working harder in place. He is built to do the second — it will feel like the right instrument and will be the wrong one. That is already listed among his blind spots.

23 The same question, from placement alone

§22 answered career and earning using Shodhya Pinda, Ashtakavarga bindus, Bhava Bala ranks, a career score, and an income score I built myself. That is a fair thing to object to. Half of those are constructed rather than classical, and a reading that only works with its own scoring is not a reading.

So verify_placement.py throws all of it away. Nothing in this section uses Shadbala, Ashtakavarga, Shodhya Pinda, Bhava Bala, Ishta/Kashta, Vimshopaka, or any score. What is left is what Parashara actually works from: which graha is in which house, what it rules, what condition it is in, what it aspects, and who it sits with. Everything below is countable off the chart by hand.

The test at the end is the one that matters: does the answer survive?

Where every house lord sits

This is the most informative table you can build without a number in it.

Every one of the twelve lords sits in the 8th, the 9th or the 10th. Three houses out of twelve receive the entire chart. You need no strength measure to see what that means: whatever happens in this life is routed through transformation, fortune and work. There is no other address.

Career, read only from placement

The 10th lord is Budha, and it sits in the 8th. Classically unambiguous: the house of career is owned by a graha standing in the house of crisis, research, hidden things and other people's resources. The work is investigative, and the career is not stable in form.

And that same graha is combust — 9.00° from Surya, inside the 14° limit. A combust 10th lord means the career significator is swallowed by the Sun: his own position obscured by the authority he stands next to. Work done under someone else's name. That is placement, not opinion.

But the 10th is not empty. Guru occupies it — Amala Yoga by placement alone, one of the few classical yogas needing no strength test: a natural benefic in the career house gives lasting reputation and clean standing. And the qualification is also pure placement — Guru is in Mithuna, its enemy's sign. The reputation is real and the instrument is uncomfortable.

§10 reports that nothing aspects the 8th and calls it the one sealed chamber in the chart. Counted properly there are two — and the second is the career house. That section has been corrected.

The 10th holds Guru and receives nothing from outside — no help, no interference, no correction. His career runs on its single occupant and on nothing else. Whatever Guru is worth in the 10th is the whole of what the career house has.

And the 2nd house is empty and receives four aspects — the most of any house in the chart. Among them Guru, the most benefic aspect in Jyotisha, and Shukra, its own lord aspecting its own house from the 8th. A house that is empty but aspected by its own lord and by Jupiter is classically well protected. No strength table shows this.

Earning, read only from placement

The 2nd lord Shukra is in the 8th. Wealth held is ruled by a graha in the house of other people's resources — money through what is transferred rather than earned in increments, arriving in events rather than in a steady line.

The 11th lord Chandra is in the 9th, exalted. Income is ruled by a graha standing exalted in the house of fortune and elders. This is a good placement, and it says something specific: gains come through seniors, mentors, teachers and well-disposed elders — through people who favour him, not through mechanism.

The yogas that are pure placement

Parivartana. The 8th lord Mangal is in the 9th and the 9th lord Shukra is in the 8th — an outright exchange. Transformation and fortune are not two departments here; they are one mechanism wearing two names, and neither can be reached except through the other.

Vimala Yoga. The 12th lord Surya stands in the 8th — a dusthana lord in a dusthana, present by placement.

Raja yoga. Budha (lagna and 10th — kendra) conjunct Shukra (9th — trikona), in the 8th.

Dhana combination. Chandra (11th) with Shani (5th) in the 9th. One qualification, also pure placement: Shani rules the 5th and the 6th — a trikona and a dusthana on one body. Schools differ on whether the trikona prevails or the dusthana taints it. This reading does not adjudicate; it records that the chart's income combination is built on a graha with divided loyalties.

Note where they sit. The raja yoga is in the 8th. The income combination is in the 9th. Neither is in a career house — so his elevation and his gains both arrive through the 8th–9th axis, through upheaval and through fortune, rather than through the 10th.

He is not promoted into things. He is delivered into them.

The career varga, still only placement

D10 ascendant Kumbha, lord Shani. Three grahas hold good D10 houses by placement alone: Shukra in the D10 10th itself, and Guru and Shani in D10 trikonas. And Budha — the birth chart's 10th lord — falls in a D10 dusthana, the same verdict the rashi chart gave, reached independently in the career varga.

The shape of his working life is owned by Shani — structure, patience, service, long institutional time — not by the 10th lord of the birth chart.

So does the answer survive without the numbers?

§22's four claims, tested against placement alone:

1. "The employment relation is the strong part, not the title." — SURVIVES, by a different route. The 10th lord is combust in the 8th while the D10 ascendant is owned by Shani, the graha of service inside structures. No bindu count required.

2. "Income is the weak channel." — DOES NOT SURVIVE IN THAT FORM. This is the correction. By placement the 11th lord is exalted. That is not weakness. What placement says instead is that income is conditional rather than weak: it comes through the 9th — through elders, mentors and fortune — and not through mechanism or accumulation. The score-based reading called that "weak flow"; placement calls it "flow that depends on people." Those are different claims, and placement is the more classical one.

3. "Money arrives as events, not increments." — SURVIVES CLEANLY. The 2nd lord in the 8th says it alone, and the 8th–9th exchange reinforces it. This was never a scoring claim.

4. "Raises come from moving, not waiting." — PARTLY SURVIVES, WITH A CHANGED REASON. It rested on the 6th's bindu count, now discarded. What placement supports is different: the raja yoga sits in the 8th and the income combination in the 9th, so advancement does not come through the 10th house at all. Change is still the mechanism — but because his elevation is not located in his career house, not because he wins competitions.

And one thing placement says that the scores never did. The 2nd is empty and aspected by its own lord and by Guru; the 11th lord is exalted; the 10th is sealed but holds Amala Yoga.

Read without a single strength figure, this is a better wealth chart than §22 described. The bindus were dragging the reading pessimistic.

24 A named rank in a selling organisation

The question was "when will he become diamond" — a named achievement tier in a direct-selling or sales organisation.

First, what Jyotisha cannot do with this. It cannot name a company's ladder. No chart contains the word Diamond, no chart knows one firm's tiers from another's, and any reading that hands back a date for a specific corporate rank is inventing it. That has to be said before anything else, because this is precisely the kind of question a reading gets flattering about.

What it can do is translate the question into houses, test whether the chart supports that mode of earning at all, and date the windows when the apparatus is live. Placement-first throughout, per §23's discipline — no bindu counts, no Shadbala, no constructed scores. Dasha dates are timing, not scoring.

The four houses a rank tier actually sits on

HouseSignLordLord inOccupantsAspected byUpagrahas
11gains, networks, attainmentKarkaChandra9emptyShani, KetuGulika, Mandi
3initiative, persuasionVrischikaMangal9KetuChandra, Mangal, Shani, Rahu—
7the public, tradeMeenaGuru10emptyKetu onlyUpaketu
10standing, the titleMithunaBudha8Gurunothing—

The kama trikona — and this is the finding

The 3rd, 7th and 11th are the kama trikona, the three houses of desire, ambition and attainment. They are precisely the machinery a rank ladder runs on.

The only occupant of all three houses of desire is Ketu — the graha of detachment, severance and the removal of wanting. The one body in the sky whose function is to make a thing stop mattering. And it sits alone in his houses of ambition, in the 3rd — the house of going out and asking — in the severest gandanta pada.

This is not a new finding; it is the mechanism §48 identified when asked why he walks away from what he wanted most. What is new is that the rank question lands exactly on it.

And the rest of the trikona is thin in the same direction. The 7th is empty, aspected by nothing but Ketu, and contains Upaketu — §54 called it the weakest signature in the chart. The 11th is empty of grahas and carries both Gulika and Mandi, the two harshest upagrahas in the scheme; it receives Shani's aspect and Ketu's, and no benefic reaches it. The 3rd receives four aspects and three of the four are malefic.

All three houses of desire are empty, shadow-occupied, or reached only by malefics. That is a coherent structure, and it is not an encouraging one for a ladder built on recruiting people.

But the 11th lord is exalted — so what is the real reading?

The counterweight is real, and §23 found it: Chandra, the 11th lord, stands exalted in the 9th. A weak house with an exalted lord is a specific configuration, not a contradiction.

The house is poorly built — the machinery of networks and tiers. The lord is well placed — so the gains themselves are strong, and they arrive from where the lord sits. Chandra sits in the 9th: fortune, elders, teachers, mentors, people senior to him who are well disposed toward him.

His gains come through people who respect him. Not through people he recruits.

A rank conferred by a network he has to build sits on his weakest structure. A position handed to him by a senior who rates him sits on an exalted lord in the house of elders — the same conclusion §21 reached from a completely different direction when it found he becomes the one things are handed to, rather than the one whose name is on the door.

When the apparatus is actually live

Chandra matured at 24, in April 2026 — the 11th lord is already mature, and it is the only one of the four relevant lords that is. The gains instrument is available now in a way it was not two years ago.

WindowWhat it supplies
Rahu–GuruSep 2025 – Jan 2028Guru — karaka of expansion and groups, and lord of the 7th
Rahu–ChandraJun 2038 – Dec 2039the 11th lord's own antardasha
Guru–GuruDec 2040 – Feb 2043Guru mahadasha opens — sixteen years of expansion
Guru–ShukraNov 2048 – Jul 2051Shukra under Guru

Read that honestly, including the first row. Rahu–Guru is running now, to January 2028, and Guru is the natural karaka of expansion and groups and rules the 7th. The current window is not dead for this. It is karaka support without lord support — which classically shows as a good period to begin and a poor one to expect the tier from.

A start is supported now; the tier is not dated before 2038.

The gains apparatus does not come under its own lord's period until June 2038, with the real expansion from December 2040 — which §43 had already called the step, by an entirely different route.

And the stretch in between, 2028 to 2033, is pointed somewhere else entirely: at the career foundation (2028–30) and at identity (2030–33). Effort routed into a rank ladder across those years is competing with what the chart is actually doing.

What the chart offers instead

  1. The mechanism is backwards for him. A tier ladder pays for recruiting breadth. His chart pays for being rated by seniors. The same effort routed through mentors, institutions and people who already respect his work converts; routed into building a downline it runs into an empty 11th with both harsh upagrahas in it.
  2. The 6th and the D10 say employment. §54's finding stands — the house of service is his strongest, the house of independent trade his weakest. A rank inside an employer's structure sits on completely different houses than a rank inside a selling network, and those are the houses he owns.
  3. And §48 is the warning. The one occupant of his three houses of desire is the graha that removes wanting. If he climbs this ladder, the structural prediction is not that he fails. It is that he arrives and stops caring. That is worth knowing before the effort rather than after it.

The limits, plainly

  1. No chart names a corporate rank. What is dated above is the activation of the houses such a rank belongs to — a different and weaker claim.
  2. This does not say he cannot. Charts describe where effort converts cheaply and where it converts expensively. A weak 11th means the ladder costs him more per rung than it costs someone else — not that the rung is unreachable. People succeed against their charts constantly.
  3. And if he has already started, the reading changes shape. Everything above is about whether to route effort this way. If he is already in it, the useful parts are the dates and the warning — not the structural verdict, which is behind him.

25 Bhanga — the cancellation rules, tested

"Are there any cancel-out rules for the charts given" is a better question than it looks, and this document had largely not answered it. A search of the reading returns no neecha bhanga, no Kuja dosha, no papakartari, no yoga bhanga. Kemadruma is mentioned five times and never actually tested.

One item in that gap is substantial: Mangal dosha had never been computed at all, despite three sections on marriage. It is the single question families actually ask before a match.

Jyotisha's cancellation doctrines come in four families, and they work in opposite directions:

A dosha is cancelledan affliction annulled by a counter-condition — Kuja dosha bhanga, Kemadruma bhanga
A weakness is cancelleddebilitation or combustion undone — neecha bhanga, combustion exemptions
A yoga is cancelleda combination forms but cannot deliver — yoga bhanga
A yoga requires afflictionand is spoiled by strength — the viparita raja yogas

Every rule below is stated before it is applied, so the reasoning can be checked rather than taken. Where schools disagree, the disagreement is reported rather than resolved.

Two doctrines that are moot before we start

Neecha bhanga. The rule rescues a debilitated graha when its dispositor sits in a kendra, or the graha exalted in that sign does, or the dispositor aspects it. Debilitated grahas in the rashi chart: none. Every graha is exalted, own, friendly, neutral or in an enemy sign — not one is fallen. For a chart carrying this much load that is worth stating plainly. There is nothing to rescue.

Combustion exemption. Combustion is annulled when the combust graha is retrograde, in its own sign, or exalted.

Budha, combust at 9.00° from Surya
retrograde?No — speed +2.0609, direct
own sign?No — in Mesha
exalted?No

No exemption applies. The combustion of the lagna and 10th lord stands uncancelled — and since no graha in this chart is retrograde, no other body claims the exemption either.

Kuja dosha — computed here for the first time

The rule. Mangal in the 1st, 2nd, 4th, 7th, 8th or 12th afflicts marriage, reckoned from the lagna, from Chandra, and in many South Indian traditions also from Shukra.

Reckoned fromMangal stands in
Lagnathe 9thclean
Chandrathe 1stdosha
Shukrathe 2nddosha

Present from Chandra and Shukra, absent from the lagna — the single most common configuration in real charts, and exactly why the doctrine is argued about. From the lagna, the reference most schools weight heaviest, he is clean.

And then the cancellations.

Cancellation rule
1. Mangal own sign or exaltedNo — Vrishabha, neutral
2. conjunct or aspected by Guru or ChandraYES — conjunct Chandra
3. conjunct or aspected by ShaniYES — conjunct
5. in a kendra from GuruNo — 12th from Guru
6. partner carries the same doshaUnknowable — not supplied

Two cancellations fire, and both are strong ones. Mangal is conjunct Chandra — and Chandra is exalted there — and conjunct Shani. Rule 2 is the most widely accepted cancellation in the whole doctrine, and it applies here in its strongest form: not a mere aspect but a conjunction, with the Moon in its exaltation sign.

Kuja dosha is absent from the lagna, present from Chandra and Shukra, and cancelled by conjunction with an exalted Chandra and with Shani.

Note what that does not say. It does not say the marriage is unafflicted — §54 found the 7th empty, aspected only by Ketu and holding Upaketu, and none of that involves Mangal. It says the specific dosha people ask about is not the problem in this chart.

Kemadruma does not form

The rule. Kemadruma arises when no graha occupies the 2nd or 12th from Chandra, and none is conjunct it — isolation, and support that does not arrive.

SignOccupants
2nd from ChandraMithunaGuru
12th from ChandraMeshaSurya, Budha, Shukra
conjunct ChandraVrishabhaMangal, Shani, Rahu

It does not form, and it is not close. All three positions are occupied. The chart's most-cited yoga of isolation is comprehensively absent.

Papakartari — none, and for a structural reason

The rule. A house hemmed between malefics — one in the house before and one in the house after — is under papakartari, scissors affliction.

No house in this chart is under papakartari. The 9th shows a shubhakartari (benefic enclosure) — Budha and Shukra before, Guru after — but it is qualified rather than clean, because Surya sits in the 8th too, so the enclosure is mixed.

This follows directly from the stellium. Seven grahas occupy two adjacent houses, so ten of the twelve houses have at least one empty neighbour and cannot be hemmed at all. The concentration that weakens this chart in other ways protects it completely from enclosure affliction.

Yoga bhanga — the family that actually bites here

The rule, and it is the least discussed of the four. A yoga can form perfectly and still fail to deliver. The classical spoilers: its grahas stand in a dusthana; a participant is combust; a participant is in an enemy sign; its lords also rule dusthanas.

YogaLocatedCondition of participants
Raja — Budha (1st, 10th) with Shukra (2nd, 9th)8th — a dusthanaBudha neutral, COMBUST; Shukra neutral
Amala — Guru in the 10th10thGuru in enemy sign
Vimala — Surya, the 12th lord, in the 8th8th — a dusthanaSurya exalted

The raja yoga is spoiled in two ways at once: it sits in the 8th, and one of its two participants is combust. It is a genuine raja yoga — kendra lord with trikona lord, no argument — standing in the crisis house with its kendra lord swallowed by the Sun.

This is the central yoga bhanga of the chart, and it is the structural reason a reading that keeps finding raja yogas keeps also finding they do not simply pay out.

Amala is not cancelled but it is diminished — Guru is in the 10th, which is all Amala requires, but in its enemy's sign. The yoga stands; the graha is uncomfortable.

And the yoga that may be cancelled by strength

The rule inverts everything above. The viparita raja yogas — Harsha, Sarala, Vimala — arise when a dusthana lord stands in a dusthana. Their whole logic is that a bad house's ruler, badly placed, cancels its own harm. The yoga therefore depends on the lord being spoiled.

A significant body of opinion holds that a viparita yoga is itself cancelled when the dusthana lord is strong — because a graha in good condition does not ruin the house it rules, and there is nothing for the inversion to work on.

Vimala is formed: the 12th lord Surya stands in the 8th. But Surya is exalted, vargottama, and the best-dignified graha in the chart.

By the formation rule it is present. By the affliction rule it is cancelled — an exalted 12th lord is not a spoiled one, and viparita needs spoilage.

This reading does not adjudicate. It records that the chart's one viparita yoga rests on a graha too well placed to satisfy the doctrine that produces it — and notes that §9 already found Vimala dissolving under every cuspal house frame, by a completely different mechanism. Two independent routes to doubting the same yoga is worth recording.

What the cancellation pass changes

  1. Kuja dosha — the biggest practical result, and a favourable one. Not an obstacle.
  2. Kemadruma — absent, comprehensively.
  3. Papakartari — none, for a structural reason.
  4. Neecha bhanga — does not arise; no graha is debilitated.
  5. Combustion — not cancelled. The weakness of the lagna and 10th lord stands exactly as the reading has always described it.
  6. Yoga bhanga — the raja yoga is spoiled twice, Amala is diminished, and Vimala is doubtful on the doctrine's own logic.

The afflictions in this chart cancel. The yogas do not.

Every classical dosha tested comes back annulled or absent; every benefic combination comes back qualified. That is an unusual and quite specific result — a chart protected from the standard misfortunes and made to work for its advantages. It is the same asymmetry the reading keeps meeting from every other direction.

26 The navamsa, read as a chart

§11 prints the D9 alongside the other fifteen Shodashavarga charts — a placement list with house class and dignity. That is everything this document has ever done with it. For the varga that ranks second only to the rashi chart, and governs the spouse, dharma, the second half of life and the real inner strength of every graha, a table row is not enough.

verify_navamsa.py reads it as a chart: its own lagna, its own house lords, dignity gained and lost between D1 and D9, yogas forming inside it, and two classical points the reading had never computed.

One methodological note. Applying Parashari drishti inside a divisional chart is done by many and rejected by others, on the grounds that a varga maps dignity rather than a sky. Nothing load-bearing below depends on it.

The first fact, and this document has never stated it

D1 lagnaKanya
D9 lagnaKanya

The ascendant is vargottama. §11 prints it in a table without remarking on it.

Classically, and without any strength measure: the person is the same person in the inner chart as in the outer one. The self does not change shape between what is presented and what is actually there. A vargottama lagna gives steadiness of identity, resilience, and specifically that the second half of life confirms the first rather than contradicting it.

For a chart this full of tension between measures, it is the single most stabilising thing in it.

And note who sits on it: Rahu occupies the D9 lagna — and runs the mahadasha to December 2040. The current eighteen years are governed by the graha placed on the inner self, which is a structural reason the reading keeps describing this period as identity-level rather than circumstantial.

The navamsa as a chart

D9 lagna Kanya, lord Budha — in D9 house 11, Karka, enemy sign.

D9 houseSignLordLord inOccupants
1KanyaBudha11Rahuself
2TulaShukra3—wealth, speech
3VrischikaMangal7Shukraeffort
4DhanuGuru6—home, mother
5MakaraShani10Chandrachildren, mind
6KumbhaShani10Guruservice, illness

Compare that with §23's finding about the rashi chart, where all twelve lords crowd into just three houses. In the navamsa they scatter across seven. The outer chart is concentrated; the inner chart is distributed.

What each graha gains and loses between D1 and D9

This is the navamsa's classical job: a graha strong outside and weak inside promises more than it delivers.

GrahaD1D9
Suryaexaltedexaltedunchanged — VARGOTTAMA
Chandraexaltedneutralweaker inside
Mangalneutralfriendstronger inside
Budhaneutralenemyweaker inside
Guruenemyneutralstronger inside
Shukraneutralneutralunchanged
Shanifriendfriendunchanged

Two of those four shifts change claims already in this document.

Chandra is exalted in D1 and only neutral in D9 — the classic promises-more-than-it-delivers pattern, and it lands on a claim made two sections ago. §24 leaned on the 11th lord being exalted as the counterweight to a badly built 11th house. The navamsa qualifies that. The exaltation is real outside and does not hold up inside, so the gains-through-elders channel is a genuine promise that thins on delivery. §24 is not overturned — the placement is still the best thing the 11th has — but it was stated more confidently than the navamsa supports.

Budha is neutral in D1 and enemy in D9. The lagna and 10th lord is combust outside and uncomfortable inside. It gets worse, not better — there is no chart in the pair where the instrument of self is at ease.

And the two that improve matter too. Guru rises from enemy to neutral, so the marriage and home significator is better inside than out. Mangal rises from neutral to friend — which bears directly on the next finding.

Vargottama, computed: grahas — Surya alone; lagna — yes.

A graha both exalted and vargottama is about as well-made as Parashari dignity gets, and this chart has exactly one. It is the 12th lord, standing in the 8th. The best-built thing in the chart rules loss and sits in crisis.

The D9 seventh — the point of the varga

D9 7th signMeena
D9 7th lordGuru — in D9 house 6, a dusthana
D9 7th holdsMangal, Ketu

Two malefics occupy the navamsa seventh. §11 lists both placements and never puts them side by side. Read as a chart they are one configuration: the house of the spouse, in the varga of the spouse, holding the graha of severance and the graha of friction and nothing else. And its lord — the same Guru that rules the D1 7th — falls in a D9 dusthana.

With one mitigation the dignity table supplies: Mangal is in Meena, a friendly sign, and is one of only two grahas that gain dignity in the navamsa. A well-dignified malefic in the 7th is not the same as an afflicted one — friction that is competent rather than chaotic. Ketu carries no dignity either way.

What this does not overturn: the D1 7th is Meena at SAV 33 and Bhava rank 4; the Upapada falls in the rank-2 4th; the 8th from the Upapada is too weak to dissolve the marriage; and Kuja dosha is cancelled (§25).

The outer chart says the marriage is structurally sound. The inner chart says it is emotionally austere. Those are not contradictory — they are the two halves of what this reading has called durable and undemonstrative from the beginning. The D9 is where the "undemonstrative" comes from, and until now that word was never actually sourced.

The navamsa carries no yoga at all

Computed within D9: no kendra lord shares a house with a trikona lord. No exchange.

Set that against the rashi chart, which has both — Budha with Shukra in the 8th, and the 8th/9th parivartana. The outer chart is full of combinations and the inner chart is bare.

Classically that reads one way: what he has is not seconded from the inside. The rashi yogas are real — §25 shows exactly how they are spoiled — but a chart whose navamsa confirmed its rashi chart would deliver more easily than this one does.

The 64th navamsa — never computed here

The rule. From the navamsa occupied by the Moon, and separately from the lagna's navamsa, the 64th navamsa forward is a classical sensitive point — the Khara, or harsh, navamsa. Its lord, the Khareshi, is watched. Counting inclusively, that is 63 navamsas on; each navamsa advances the navamsa-sign by one, and 63 mod 12 = 3, so it is the 4th sign from the navamsa occupied.

FromNavamsa64th navamsaKhareshi
ChandraMakaraMeshaMangal
LagnaKanyaDhanuGuru

A correction to the draft. I had written that both Khareshis are the same graha. They are not, and the computation says so plainly. The true version is more pointed, because of what those two grahas rule:

Mangal, the Khareshi from the mind, rules the 3rd and 8th — the graha whose mahadasha ran ages 13.7 to 20.7, the transformation already lived.

Guru, the Khareshi from the body, rules the 4th and 7th — the marriage, which is the thing ahead.

One points at what is done. The other points at what is coming. Neither is a prediction; both are markers the tradition says to watch, and they are watching the two things this reading has spent the most time on.

The second half of life

The navamsa is weighted more heavily after about 35.

Shani in the D9 tenth is the placement that matters most for the later decades, and it is one of the four career credentials the reading has cited since §45. Here is where it actually comes from: the graha of structure occupies the career house of the chart that governs the second half of life. Its own mahadasha opens December 2056.

And the D9 lagna lord is Budha, in an enemy sign. Whatever else the second half improves, the instrument of self does not become easy.

What the deep pass adds

  1. The lagna is vargottama — never stated, and the most stabilising fact in the chart.
  2. Rahu occupies the D9 lagna and runs the mahadasha to 2040.
  3. The D9 7th holds Mangal and Ketu, its lord in a D9 dusthana — the source of "undemonstrative".
  4. The navamsa contains no raja yoga and no exchange, where the rashi chart has both.
  5. Chandra weakens inside, qualifying §24's counterweight.
  6. The two Khareshis are the lords of the 8th and the 7th — what is done, and what is coming.

The outer chart is crowded, contradictory and full of combinations. The inner chart is steady, bare and austere.

A vargottama lagna on an empty-handed navamsa is a person who is exactly what he appears to be, holding fewer cards than the outer chart suggests.

27 House nature — every scheme, assembled

The words kendra, trikona, upachaya and dusthana appear 237 times in this document and are never once set out as a system. They are used as adjectives. And two whole classification families are missing altogether — maraka, with zero mentions in the entire reading, and panapara / apoklima, the succedent and cadent houses, also zero. For this chart the second omission turns out to matter a great deal.

There are six schemes, not one, and they overlap. A house is never just one thing.

Scheme
By anglekendra 1 4 7 10 · panapara 2 5 8 11 · apoklima 3 6 9 12strength of position
By benefittrikona 1 5 9 · upachaya 3 6 10 11 · dusthana 6 8 12quality of result
By purusharthadharma 1 5 9 · artha 2 6 10 · kama 3 7 11 · moksha 4 8 12which aim of life
By mortalitymaraka 2 7classical difficulty markers
By obstructionbadhaka — the 7th, for a dual lagnawhat blocks
By modalitychara / sthira / dwiswabhava, from the signhow it moves

Lagna Kanya — a dwiswabhava sign, so the badhaka is the 7th.

Where the nine grahas fall

Scheme
kendra1Guru
panapara3Surya, Budha, Shukra
apoklima5Chandra, Mangal, Shani, Rahu, Ketu
trikona4Chandra, Mangal, Shani, Rahu

dusthana3Surya, Budha, Shukra
maraka0—

This is the result nobody has stated.

One graha in a kendra. Five in the cadent houses.

The kendras are the pillars — visible action, standing, effect on the world. The apoklima houses are the weakest angular position in the scheme; grahas there act late, indirectly, and off the main axis.

Eight of nine grahas sit outside the kendras. This document has noted "one kendra occupied" exactly once, in passing, as the reason no Panchamahapurusha yoga can form. It never drew the consequence:

He has almost no direct angular leverage on the world.

That single fact underwrites half of what the reading has found by other routes — responsibility without title, authority located out of sight, results arriving through the 8th–9th axis rather than the 10th, and advancement that has to be built rather than occupied.

The purushartha split

Every graha sits in exactly one of the four aims, so this is a clean partition of the nine.

HousesCount
Dharma1 5 9meaning, ethics, the father, fortune4
Artha2 6 10resources, work, the material1
Kama3 7 11desire, relationship, attainment1
Moksha4 8 12release, endings, the interior3

Seven of nine grahas sit in dharma and moksha. One in artha. One in kama.

And note which one is in kama: Ketu, the graha that removes wanting, alone in the 3rd. §24 found that when asked about a rank ladder. Here it turns out to be part of a much larger pattern.

The chart is not merely thin on desire. It is weighted seven to two toward meaning and release over acquisition and wanting — and that was sitting in the classification the whole time.

The overlaps, where the schemes earn their keep

HoLabelsOccupants
6apoklima · upachaya · dusthana · arthaempty
10kendra · upachaya · arthaGuru
8panapara · dusthana · mokshaSurya, Budha, Shukra
9apoklima · trikona · dharmaChandra, Mangal, Shani, Rahu
3apoklima · upachaya · kamaKetu
1kendra · trikona · dharmaempty

The 6th is the interesting one — apoklima, upachaya, dusthana and artha at once. A house that is simultaneously a place of suffering and a place that improves with time, whose aim is material. That is exactly why the reading keeps finding the 6th doing useful work — 41 bindus, the D10 ascendant sign — while also calling it a dusthana. Both labels are correct and they describe the same mechanism: adversity that compounds into competence.

The 9th is apoklima and trikona together, holding four grahas: the most fortunate class of house in the weakest angular position. Fortune that is real but does not act directly.

And the 10th is kendra and upachaya at once. His career house is both a pillar and a slow-builder. It does not arrive; it accrues.

What each graha is for this lagna

House nature also determines what a graha is, because a graha takes its character from what it rules. Rules stated before applying them: trikona lords are benefic; dusthana lords malefic; lords of 3 and 11 malefic by lordship; a natural benefic ruling two kendras takes kendradhipati dosha and behaves as a malefic; the lagna lord is benefic regardless.

GrahaRulesVerdict
Surya12dusthanamalefic — dusthana lord
Chandra11upachayamalefic by lordship
Mangal3, 8upachaya; dusthanamalefic — dusthana lord
Budha1, 10trikona+kendra; kendrabenefic — lagna lord (kendra dosha contested)
Guru4, 7kendra; kendraMALEFIC — kendradhipati dosha
Shukra2, 9neutral; trikonabenefic — trikona lord, no dosha
Shani5, 6trikona; dusthanamixed

Guru is a functional malefic for this lagna. It rules the 4th and 7th, both kendras, and is a natural benefic — full kendradhipati dosha. The reading already lists this as one of Amala Yoga's six qualifications (§14), but has never stated it as what it is: the chart's great benefic is, by lordship, working against him. And Guru rules his marriage and his home.

Shukra is the cleanest benefic he has — one trikona, no kendra, no dusthana, no dosha of any kind. It is also the Atmakaraka and carries the best net balance in the chart.

For a Kanya lagna, Shukra is the graha to trust — and this reading has been circling that conclusion from six directions without ever stating the lordship reason.

Maraka — zero mentions until now

The rule. The 2nd and 7th are maraka houses and their lords are marakas. This reading declines longevity (§57) and continues to decline it; what follows is structural only, with no application to lifespan.

SignLordOccupants
2ndTulaShukraempty
7thMeenaGuruempty

Both maraka houses are empty. And their lords are the two grahas just identified as the extremes of the functional scale — Shukra, the cleanest benefic, and Guru, the one carrying kendradhipati dosha.

The 7th is also the badhaka for a dual lagna, so it carries maraka, badhaka, kendra and kama simultaneously — four labels. §24 found it empty, aspected by nothing but Ketu, holding Upaketu. It is the most heavily classified and least occupied house in the chart.

What the census says that nothing else does

  1. One graha in a kendra, five in apoklima. No direct angular leverage — the structural root of "responsibility without title".
  2. Seven of nine in dharma and moksha, one each in artha and kama, and the one in kama is the graha that removes wanting.
  3. The 6th carries four labels at once — which is why it reads as both his weakest class of house and his strongest measured one.
  4. Guru is a functional malefic here, and it rules his home and his marriage. Shukra takes no dosha at all.
  5. Both maraka houses are empty, their lords the best and worst of his functional grahas.

A chart with almost nothing on its pillars, almost nothing in its houses of wanting, and almost everything in the houses of meaning and release.

Which is the same sentence this reading reached from Ashtakavarga, from Jaimini, from the navamsa and from the dasha sequence. The classification gets there with no arithmetic at all.

28 The current transit, verified and read

He supplied a live transit table. This document's discipline with supplied data is to verify it first — §2 did that with the birth data and found a constant ayanamsa offset — so the same is done here before a word of interpretation.

Verifying it

The Moon fixes the moment: it moves about 13° a day, so a match to the arcminute pins the time to within a couple of minutes. Searching 18–24 August 2026 for a Moon at 227.07 sidereal returns one answer:

21 August 2026, 12:42 IST. Which is today.

BodySuppliedComputedDiffSign
Surya124.03124.040.5′Simha
Chandra227.07227.070.0′Vrischika
Mangal72.2972.290.2′Mithuna
Budha117.41117.410.2′Karka
Guru107.18107.190.3′Karka
Shukra169.75169.760.4′Kanya
Shani349.97349.980.3′Meena
Rahu305.63305.640.5′Kumbha
Ketu125.63125.640.5′Simha

Worst disagreement across all nine: 0.5 arcminutes. Uranus and Neptune reproduce equally well. Pluto is out by 22.8′ — too large for rounding; different sources use different Pluto models, and it changes nothing here. The outer planets are not used below. Parashari jyotisha works with nine grahas; Uranus, Neptune and Pluto have no rulership, no aspect doctrine, no dasha, and no place in any technique this reading applies.

And one row does not verify.

supplied Lagna83.90 — Mithuna 23°54′
Guntur ascendant at 12:42 IST219.44 — Vrischika 9.44°

That is most of a chart apart. Working backwards: an ascendant of 83.90 occurs at Guntur at 03:02 IST, ten hours earlier, when the Moon would read 222.27 rather than 227.07. Solving instead for a longitude that would give that ascendant at this instant returns −65° — the western Atlantic.

So the ascendant row does not belong to the same moment as the planets. Some transit pages render a live planetary table beside an ascendant computed for a stored location or a stale timestamp.

And it does not matter for anything below. Gochara is read from the natal Moon and the natal lagna. The transit ascendant plays no part in it. The one row that cannot be verified is the one row the technique does not use.

Where the transits fall

Natal lagna Kanya; natal Chandra in Vrishabha. Each transit weighted by the transiting graha's own bindus in the sign it occupies — the Ashtakavarga method, and the thing that separates a transit reading from a list of positions. Threshold is 4.

GrahaTransit signDignityFrom lagnaFrom MoonBindusGochara
SuryaSimhaown12th4th4not favourable
ChandraVrischikadebilitated3rd7th4favourable
MangalMithunaenemy10th2nd4not favourable
BudhaKarkaenemy11th3rd2not favourable
